| T407 | Plan-035 Phase 1 (Layer 2) — first Terminal-Bench delta measurement, closed after a long execution saga across three distinct designs. (1) Original k=3 single-pass run (2026-08-14, real, `$34.13`): measured *within-run* trial variance (3 attempts/task inside one Harbor job), not the *across-run* spread the frozen decision rule (plan-035 §2.4) actually requires — correctly reported as an explicit non-classification rather than forced into a label, preserved verbatim in `tb-delta-v6.12.0.md` §§1-5. (2) "Design A" redesign (3 independent full-subset passes, `-k 1 -l 25`, both arms) dispatched twice from `10.10.160.12` against a remote Docker daemon on `10.10.160.11`; both attempts hit 100% trial failure (`RewardFileNotFoundError`/`AgentTimeoutError`) — root cause confirmed by direct filesystem inspection on both hosts: Docker Compose bind mounts resolve against the daemon's filesystem, not the client's, silently stranding all agent/verifier output on `.11`. (One of these two attempts was additionally halted by genuine host-memory exhaustion on `.11`, later resized 4GB→24GB by the user; independent re-verification during closeout corrected an earlier claim that this attempt's Arm A data was "valid" — it was not, same bind-mount root cause, discovered later.) (3) Fix: ran the Harbor CLI co-located with the Docker daemon, directly on `.11` (fresh Harbor 0.22.0 via a `uv`-managed Python 3.12 venv); a clean 4-trial smoke test (`$0.32`) was followed by a full, successful 3-pass Design A run (150 real trials, `$43.96`, completed 2026-09-08T05:10Z). Every number independently re-verified twice against the raw scorecard JSONs (once by the dispatching session, once again by the closing session, directly on `10.10.160.11` before any artifact copy) rather than trusted from either the runner's own labels or a prior session's self-report. Arm A pass means `[0.36, 0.28, 0.28]` → spread = **8.0pp** (exactly at the rule's own `≥ 8pp` threshold, inclusive of equality); Arm B `[0.32, 0.28, 0.28]` → spread = 4.0pp; aggregate delta (B−A) = −1.33pp. **Classification: INCONCLUSIVE** per the rule's literal text ("either arm's spread ≥ 8pp... Do not report as null") — not a Null, Positive, or Negative result; Arm A's spread alone is dispositive, Arm B's tighter spread and the small aggregate delta are not load-bearing and are not used to argue toward Null. Total real cost across T407's entire history: **≈$100** (corrected 2026-09-08 from an earlier ≈$78.41 total that misread `cost_usd: null` on the two failed Design A attempts' 181 real trial-attempts as "$0 spent" rather than "$0 recorded" — the same bind-mount bug that stranded reward files also stranded Harbor's per-trial cost bookkeeping for those trials; $78.41 remains the exact portion Harbor's own scorecards actually recorded, and a rate-based estimate from the k=3 run's own cost/elapsed-time ratio puts the unrecorded portion at ≈$22 more, rounded because it is an approximation, not a measurement — see `tb-delta-v6.12.0.md` §7.2 for full methodology, a ~2x sensitivity range depending on which elapsed-time basis is used, and the recommendation to check the Anthropic Console for the authoritative figure). Design A's 3 successful passes' scorecard JSONs and `.runs/` proof artifacts were copied from `10.10.160.11` into this worktree for path-consistency with every prior T407 artifact; the much larger raw `jobs/` trial directories (161MB on `.11`) were left there, location documented, matching this task's existing untracked-`jobs/`-tree precedent. | T513 | Investigated and fixed a real, user-reported bug: `@context-retriever` MCP server showed "Connection closed" in this repo, in the CWSO project, and on other platforms like GitHub Copilot, after `scripts/install.sh --update`. Followed the mandatory `systematic-debugging` skill -- root cause investigation before any fix. **Three compounding, independent, real gaps, each reproduced directly before being fixed, none assumed**: (1) neither `context-retriever` nor `security-audit`'s declared Python dependency (the `mcp` SDK, pinned in each server's own `requirements-mcp-server.txt`) was ever installed anywhere -- confirmed via `ModuleNotFoundError` running the exact command `.mcp.json` declares; (2) the structural root cause: `scripts/install.sh` only ever copied platform config/knowledge projections into a target project, never `implementation/runtime/memory/` or `implementation/runtime/security/`, the actual Python source these two servers' `python3 -m ...` commands need to import -- confirmed directly (CWSO has no `implementation/` directory at all), meaning these servers could only ever have worked inside the emage.code source repo itself, never in any real downstream install; (3) `context-retriever` additionally needs a per-project search index built once (`build.py`), which cannot be pre-built or shipped. Fixed all three: new `install_mcp_server_runtime()` step in `install_common()` copying both runtime directories into every target project on install and `--update`, platform-agnostically; explicit, actionable post-install notices for the pip-install and index-build steps instead of a generic client-side error days later. **A real, separate leak bug found and fixed while building this fix, caught by testing against a fresh throwaway target before trusting it**: the naive fresh-install copy path would have shipped this repo's own locally-built `_index/` into every fresh target project -- fixed by teaching `copy_tree_into()` the same exclude-path mechanism `sync_tree_into()` already had for `--update`, generalized to handle directories (not just single files) via `cp -r`. **A second, separate real bug found by CI, not assumed fixed by local testing alone**: MR !361's own first commit passed every local test (rsync present on this dev host) but failed CI's `unit-tests` job outright (`python:3.12-alpine`, no `rsync` installed) -- root cause traced via a real `bash -x` trace, both in CI's log and independently reproduced locally by hiding `rsync` from `PATH`: a bare `[[ cond ]] && cmd` as a function's own last statement made the *function's* exit status that of the failed test whenever the condition was false (true for every pre-existing caller), and `set -e` killed the whole script at the call site. Fixed by replacing all four occurrences with real `if`/`fi` blocks. Re-verified by reproducing the exact failure locally first (identical `bash -x` signature to CI's), then confirming the fix resolves it under that same no-rsync `PATH`, not just with `rsync` present. **Both real, currently-in-use environments (this repo and the user's separate CWSO project) fixed and independently confirmed working, not just the code**: dependencies installed, indexes built, `context_retriever_mcp_server`/`audit_server` both start cleanly in each; `tests/functional/test_context_retriever_mcp_server.py` 8/8 pass for real (previously skipped); `tests/functional/test_audit_server.py` 30/31 pass for real (the 1 skip is an unrelated missing `pip-audit` binary). **A separate, distinct bug found but deliberately not fixed in this task, flagged instead**: `servers.yaml` hardcodes the context-retriever index directory name as `em-age-emage.code` for every target project including CWSO -- does not break connectivity (a server only needs *an* index to exist at that path) but is semantically wrong and would collide if per-project index identity ever mattered; left as an unscoped follow-up, not silently redesigned. Full verification bar green: `tests/run.py` 734 tests `OK`; `sync.mjs --check` no drift; `check-maturity.py` 79/79; `validate-tasks.py` PASS | top-level session | 2026-09-19 | `scripts/install.sh` (modified: new `install_mcp_server_runtime()` step, `copy_tree_into()`/`sync_tree_into()` exclude-path + directory-safety fixes, post-install notices, `set -e`-safe conditionals); MR !361 (merge `863ed82`, commits `3676de2`/`48b597f`); this repo's and CWSO's own local environments fixed live (dependencies installed, indexes built); ledger rows in this commit |

| T515 | Contract authority for the six `tracked_defect` golden cases (`plan-064` Phase 9, first task). **Deliberately scoped read-only and split decide-then-implement**, departing from `plan-064` §2.8's written first step: scoping found each defect case is one half of a *zero-sum pair* -- every affected command also has a sibling `expected_pass` case whose `expect.py` encodes the same contract against an opposite fixture, so amending a contract to match the real corpus relocates the failure rather than removing it. A side effect is that this task needed **no** `protected-paths-v1.md` §5 authorization at all, which §2.8 had assumed the first Phase 9 task would. **Principle decided**: *a command's declared contract is authoritative over the corpus unless the contract contradicts a higher-authority document, or the only thing in dispute is a label for content the corpus already carries -- popularity is not authority*, applied via 5 ordered branches, with a corollary table making the paired sibling's fate follow mechanically from the branch rather than being a per-case judgment. **Verdicts: 2 of 6 end green, 1 stops blocking without going green, 3 stay red** -- of the three `experimental` agents only `security-engineer` promotes; `orchestrator` and `tech-lead` do not. That non-sweep is the point: the brief flagged "all six resolve cleanly" in advance as a suspicious result rather than a target, and the implementer additionally singled out its own single promotion-unblocking verdict as *the one most likely to be motivated reasoning, check it hardest*, recording the counter-reading rather than dismissing it and explicitly saying not to promote on its word. **Owner chosen for disinterest**: Solution Architect owns zero commands, while three of the four agents owning the affected commands are the same agents a favourable resolution would promote -- a structural control, since unlike `T514` no script adjudicates here. **Independently re-verified by the top-level session, not accepted on self-report**: the implementer had no shell tool, so its corpus surveys were sampled file-by-file and disclosed as such; all four were re-run exhaustively -- `## Task Decomposition` 0/68, `## Resource Assignments` 0/68, all six headers in order 0/68, `[CHECKPOINT] id=` 0/57, `## RELEASE VERDICT` 0/57 checkpoints and 0/33 releases (both candidate homes), `-v<maj>.<min>.md` 0 of 73 -- every conclusion held and **one figure was found materially understated** (`## Goal` 1/8 sampled vs **29/68 (43%)** actual, which does not change the verdict since all six in order remains 0/68). The three load-bearing claims checked directly against source: `AGENTS.md:27` mandates `checkpoint-<SEQ>-<phase>.md` while `new-feature.md:31` mints `checkpoint-feature-<slug>.md`; `plan.md:16` says `docs/plans/<slug>-plan.md` while `plan.md:35` says `docs/plans/plan-<ID>.md` (the command contradicts itself); `security-guidelines.md:22`/`:182-183` independently require `CRITICAL` to block merge, so that rule genuinely cannot be amended. Also verified: exactly 2 untracked files, `ADR-007` free, **zero held-out case IDs** in either output (referred to positionally as H-1/H-2, with agent-level promotion impact stated jointly so no verdict branch is pinned to a command), `tests/golden/**` and `scripts/scorecard.py` untouched. **Two findings that corrected the brief**: (1) the zero-sum premise was over-general -- in all three open pairs the siblings disagree about the **artifact class or path**, not only content (`/prepare-release`'s pair reads `fixture/docs/checkpoints/*.md` vs `fixture/release-notes.md`), an unstated disagreement about *which artifact a clause governs*, and two of the six cases have no sibling at all; (2) suite health -- these verdicts take `tracked_defect` 6 -> 3, so T411's >=5 `known_failing` floor survives but the margin narrows and Phase 9's 14 new cases must carry the replacement difficulty. **Process defect in the dispatch, owned**: the Solution Architect agent type has no `Bash`/`Grep`/`Glob`, yet the brief told it to run `git rev-parse`, commit and push. It reported this up front as a `technical`/`major` blocker instead of fabricating verification, and left the work clean and uncommitted; the top-level session committed and pushed. Recorded as a standing dispatch lesson. Full bar green: `check-maturity.py` 79/79 0 failing; `validate-tasks.py` PASS; `pytest tests/functional` 694 passed, 23 skipped | solution-architect, top-level session | 2026-09-25 | `docs/decisions/ADR-007-command-contract-authority.md` (new); `docs/artifacts/command-contract-resolution-v1.md` (new); MR !372 (merge `158ead0`); `docs/tasks/task-T515.md`; `docs/plans/plan-066-t515-command-contract-authority.md`; ledger rows in this commit |
| T516 | Narrowed the §3.5 defect check from a free-text scan to a declared field, fixing a defect in the promotion gate itself. `_ledger_defect()` decided whether a component had an open defect by regex-scanning the **entire body** of every active P0/P1 brief for the component's id with `(?<![\w-])<id>(?![\w-])`; since neither `/` nor `.` is a word character, a brief that spelled out its mandated branch name `agent/<slug>/<id>` **indicted its own owner**, and one citing `.claude/rules/git-workflow.md` demoted a `stable` instruction for the offence of being referenced. The check could not tell a reference from an accusation. **Both failures were found by running the script against a real draft (`T515`'s brief) rather than by reading the regex**, and both had stayed latent only because nearly every component was `experimental` until `T514` -- promoting 5 agents turned a dormant tooling bug into an active one. Fixed by a declared `**Affects:**` field consumed via `ctx.ledger_affects`, built once in `build_context()`. **Fail-closed three ways**, the design constraint the brief named as the one way this change could do real damage: a P0/P1 brief with no field raises `LedgerDeclarationError` and `main()` returns **exit 2 with no component report at all** (no reassuring `PASS`); `_ledger_defect()` independently raises if asked about a row absent from the map, so a hand-built `Context` cannot degrade to "affects nothing"; and malformed entries or entries naming a non-existent component are **rejected rather than ignored**, since a typo would otherwise silently protect the component it was meant to indict. The tempting "absent field means affects nothing" fallback was explicitly rejected as *worse than the bug* -- it converts a loud false positive into a silent false negative while still printing `PASS`. New ledger check **C11** enforces the field at P0/P1 (exit 1). The `Title` scan was **dropped**, justified: with the field mandatory and validated, a `Title` hit can only arise where the brief did not declare the component, so it can contribute false positives and nothing else, and a ledger table cell has no room for the rephrasing workaround a body allowed. **Judgment call the implementer got right against the brief's own wording**: the brief said to update §3.5 of `maturity-promotion-criteria-v1.md`, but that file's own second line reads *"Immutable once produced; revisions bump `<N>`"* and `AGENTS.md` says revisions create new versions, never overwrite -- so it produced **`-v2`** (v1 verbatim apart from metadata, a "Changes from v1" section, §3.5 and a `Consumed by` entry), left v1 with only a supersession banner, and flagged the deviation for review rather than silently diverging. The brief was wrong; the standing invariant outranked it. **Independently re-verified by the top-level session**: `_mentions()` absent from the diff and `test_mentions_helper_is_unchanged` passes against **both** old and new code (direct evidence criterion (d)'s second consumer `_referenced_in_agents_or_commands()` is undisturbed); `git diff -- implementation/knowledge/` empty (no `maturity:` value changed); `git diff -- tests/golden/ scripts/scorecard.py` empty; no `def test_` deleted; the pristine tree at `6a99d64` rebuilt with only the new test files dropped in and run, giving **19 failed / 21 passed**, matching the implementer's reported 13+6 and 16+5 exactly, with both headline regressions failing there and passing on the fixed tree; fail-closed behaviour re-tested by hand (stripping T515's `Affects:` line yields exit 2 with no report from the gate and exit 1 / `FAIL C11` from the validator). **The riskiest claim checked hardest**: simulating the *old* scan against `task-T515.md` showed it matched `command/plan`, `command/new-feature`, `command/prepare-release` and `agent/orchestrator`; the implementer declared the three commands and dropped `orchestrator` as a pure reference ("that is the orchestrator's job"). | T517 | Fixed a defect in **shipped output**: `sync.mjs`'s `claude-code` branch of `emitMcp()` rendered env references with VS Code's `${env:VAR}` placeholder, which Claude Code does not expand -- it expands `${VAR}`/`${VAR:-default}` in `command`, `args`, `env`, `url` and `headers` -- so every generated `.mcp.json` shipped literal, unexpanded placeholder text. Found from a **user report** that the `hindsight` MCP server would not start; **not a user misconfiguration** -- the env var was set and the generated config was wrong. **The visible half was the smaller half**: `hindsight` and `cwso` take `fromEnv` in `url` and failed loudly (`'url' is not a valid URL`), but `gitlab`, `brave` and `toolradar` take it in `env` and failed **silently** -- process starts, client reports healthy, and the literal `${env:...}` string is handed over as a credential, surfacing later as an upstream auth error with nothing pointing back at the config. Three of the five are `core`-tagged, so the defect reached **every** downstream project installing this platform, not just this repo. Fix is three call sites (`url`, `headers`, `env`) in that one branch; the architecture was already right -- `mapTemplatedValue()` takes the template per platform and `opencode` already passed a different one, so one constant was wrong, not the design. **Proven by a live MCP handshake, not by inference or unit tests.** The brief originally made "a live reconnect" the decisive criterion; the orchestrator then *wrongly* downgraded it at dispatch, asserting from assumption that no in-session reconnect was possible. The implementer disproved that: `claude mcp list` runs as a separate health-checking process and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` isolates it. **Independently reproduced from scratch by the top-level session in its own throwaway config dir before merge**: `${HINDSIGHT_MCP_URL}` -> **Connected** (real HTTP MCP handshake against the live server) vs `${env:HINDSIGHT_MCP_URL}` -> **`'url' is not a valid URL`**, same variable, same process, same minute; plus the mechanism control on an *unset* variable -- the bare form emits `Missing environment variables:` while the `env:` form emits nothing, because it is never parsed as a variable reference at all. **`gitlab` hypothesis confirmed and sharpened**: probed directly over stdio, it exits during startup with `GITLAB_API_URL contains an invalid URL: ${env:GITLAB_API_URL}` (the `CONNECTION_CLOSED` signature) and completes `initialize` with expanded values -- it was failing on the **URL**, not the token. **Scope held deliberately**: only `claude-code` was proven wrong and `${env:VAR}` is correct for at least VS Code, so the brief forbade "fixing" the other five emitters -- verified afterwards that the other 12 generated MCP files are byte-identical (`sha256sum -c`). **Tests corrected, not weakened**: `test_platform_projections.py` now selects the template per format, and the top-level session **independently mutation-proved** it -- reverted the constant, regenerated, watched the test fail, restored, and confirmed the tree bit-identical to HEAD. `test_mcp_secret_guard.py` admits a third placeholder family `^\$\{[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*\}$` that no credential shape can satisfy, with a new adversarial test keeping six literal-secret forms flagged; `${VAR:-default}` was deliberately **not** admitted. **Correction to the record, the most reusable finding**: `mcp-header-url-templating-design-v2.md` §5.3 documents that v1 cited a hand-added root `.mcp.json` as "empirically verified" evidence for the claude-code shape, when no running client had ever read that file -- it attested to the *field shape* only, never the placeholder syntax inside the fields, and **that over-reading is how `${env:VAR}` reached this branch**. v1 keeps a supersession banner only, per `AGENTS.md` immutability (the same rule `T516` hit). Also verified: no `maturity:` change, `servers.yaml` untouched, no projection hand-edited, protected paths clean, no probe residue in `~/.claude.json`, no credential-config change. Post-merge `install.sh --update` across all 7 platforms produced no further diff, and **T512's two known `install --update` regressions recurred and were caught by pre-snapshot** (`.claude/settings.json` deleted, `active-tasks.md` reset to the fresh-project scaffold) -- both restored; the underlying install.sh bug remains unfixed and is flagged as follow-up. Full bar: `tests/run.py` 762 tests OK (skipped=23), `pytest tests/functional` 722 passed (+2, none removed); `validate-tasks.py` PASS; `check-maturity.py` 79/79 0 failing; `sync.mjs --check` no drift across 577 files; `check.py --required` 7/7 | devops-engineer, top-level session | 2026-09-25 | `implementation/scripts/sync.mjs`; `.mcp.json` + `implementation/.mcp.json` (regenerated); `docs/artifacts/mcp-header-url-templating-design-v2.md` (new, 645 lines); `-v1.md` (supersession banner only); `tests/functional/test_platform_projections.py`; `tests/functional/test_mcp_secret_guard.py` (+2); MR !374 (merge `c30556d`); `docs/tasks/task-T517.md`; `docs/plans/plan-068-t517-claude-code-mcp-placeholder-syntax.md`; ledger rows in this commit |
| T518 | Stopped `scripts/install.sh --update` destroying project-local state. Two sub-defects, one trigger: `sync_tree_into()` runs `rsync -a --delete`, so any target file with no counterpart in the shipped source tree is swept. **The reported defect was the smaller half.** The brief named only `.claude/settings.json`; the implementer was told to check the other platform trees rather than fix what was reported, and found `.github` **exposed and worse** -- `implementation/.github/` ships only `agents/ hooks/ instructions/ prompts/ skills/`, so an `--update` was deleting the target project's **entire `.github/workflows/` CI**, plus `ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`, `PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE*`, `CODEOWNERS`, `dependabot.yml`, `FUNDING.yml` and `copilot-instructions.md`. This repo uses GitLab CI and so never noticed; every downstream project on GitHub Actions would have. **Independently reproduced by the top-level session before and after**: pre-fix, a seeded `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/CODEOWNERS` and `.claude/settings.json` were all deleted by `--update`; post-fix all survive, on both the rsync and the no-rsync path. Second sub-defect: `merge-task-docs.py` preserved ledger table **rows** but discarded surrounding **prose**, substituting the template's -- which asserts the ledger starts empty and the first task is `T001`, **actively false** in a repo with 316 completed tasks, in the file a cold session reads first and trusts literally. Worst precisely when the ledger is *healthy*: with zero active rows there is nothing to preserve and the template wins outright (reproduced: 65 lines -> 11, false claim 0 -> 1, while `completed-tasks.md`'s 316 rows survived). Fixed by splicing -- normalise the rows, re-emit every other line byte-for-byte, use the template only when there is no table to splice into. **A latent bug the fix would otherwise have activated**: `copy_tree_into`'s no-rsync branch did `rm -rf "$dest/$e"` unconditionally, deleting pre-existing *target* content never present in the source -- the opposite of `rsync --exclude`. Unreachable before (`.claude` passed no excludes), but adding excludes would have made a **fresh** install destructive on any host without rsync. Now guarded, and the guard proven load-bearing by removing it and watching the new test fail. **Contract decision, reviewed by the top-level session rather than accepted**: `--update` no longer pushes template *prose* into an installed project. Justification verified -- `merge_tree_preserve_existing()` (`install.sh:219`, `--ignore-existing`) already governs every other `docs/` subtree with the comment "local/project docs are never overwritten"; **`docs/tasks` was the sole exception**, so the change aligns it with the established convention rather than inventing one. Help text rewritten, since the old sentence ("preserves task rows") was satisfied *literally* by the broken behaviour. **Three tests corrected because they encoded the bug** (each asserted template prose *must* replace the target's); none weakened, each now asserts the opposite with an inline comment recording what it used to say. Pre-fix run of the new/corrected tests: 11 failed, 33 passed; post-fix 44 passed. **Reported and deliberately unfixed**: `.clinerules/` still sweeps unknown user files -- the one tree the exclude mechanism cannot express, since every entry is a bare `.md` with no reserved name distinguishing harness content from a user's own rule; a correct fix needs provenance-based pruning from `.generated-manifest.json`, which `install.sh` never reads. `.clinerules` and `.cline` were added to the `validate_before_update` warning loop, which had not mentioned them at all. Full bar: `pytest tests/functional` **734 passed** (722 baseline, +12, none removed), 23 skipped; `check-maturity.py` 79/79; `validate-tasks.py` PASS; `sync.mjs --check` no drift across 577 files | devops-engineer, top-level session | 2026-09-25 | `scripts/install.sh`; `scripts/merge-task-docs.py`; `tests/functional/test_install_script.py`; `tests/functional/test_merge_task_docs.py`; MR !378; `docs/tasks/task-T518.md`; `docs/plans/plan-069-t518-t519-carried-defects.md`; ledger rows in this commit |

Digests were **independently recomputed**, not copied from the report: `tests_golden` drifted as expected while `scripts_scorecard` stayed byte-identical to v1, which is itself cryptographic evidence `scripts/scorecard.py` was never touched. | T525 | Authored golden-suite coverage **wave 1** under the file-scoped `protected-paths-v1.md` §5 authorization in `task-T525.md` §1 — four **new** case directories under `tests/golden/open/` for `/handoff`, `/validate-tasks`, `/discover-skills` and `/skillify`, the first three of which had no golden case at all and were blocked by criterion 4 alone. **3 green, 1 red — the non-sweep shape `plan-072` §1 named in advance as the sign of honest authoring.** **The red case is the substantive finding:** `skillify.md`'s "Generate the Skill File" step declares a five-section `SKILL.md` template, and a corpus survey found **0 of 26 real skill files satisfy it — in both trees, including `.github/skills/`, the exact path the command names**. The corpus is not unstructured; it uniformly follows a *different* template that is itself declared, in the `skillify` **skill**'s own "SKILL.md Template" section. It now carries an explicit `"minItems": 0` plus a `description`, a semantic no-op that makes "unbounded below on purpose" distinguishable from "omitted by oversight" — the exact defect class that produced this task. **Second defect found: the schema had no runtime effect whatsoever.** `validator.py`, the enforcement point the security model names, **never loads `schema-v1.json`** — verified, its only mention is a docstring — and did not inspect either array at all, so `"writablePaths": "docs/**"` as a bare string passed. `minItems` alone would have changed nothing observable, so `_validate_constraint_string_list()` was added mirroring the schema exactly, with `minLength: 1` mirrored **literally** rather than the stricter `not item.strip()` — diverging in the stricter direction would recreate the two-out-of-step problem in the opposite direction. **Third finding corrects the orchestrator's own earlier reasoning:** it had found this mutation failing to flip the `handoff-payload-schema-fields-real` golden case, concluded "the check faithfully encodes the schema, my mutation was mis-designed", and moved on. **Half wrong** — `expect.py` implements `required`, `additionalProperties`, `enum`, `pattern` and `properties` and **zero array keywords**, so the mutation could never have flipped at any schema version in either direction. Also flagged rather than silently fixed: `validate_handoff_payload` already exceeded the 50-line standard, so the new logic went into a separate helper. Cycles get their own code because **the failure belongs to a set of rows rather than one**: the message names the whole chain, since no single row in a cycle is the one at fault. Self-edges are excluded from the cycle graph and left to `C13`, so each defect reports once under the more specific code; the search covers active rows only, since `completed-tasks.md` has no dependency column and an edge into it is a leaf that cannot close a cycle. `dependency_cycles()` is an iterative DFS that **slices the path from the revisited node**, yielding the chain an operator must break rather than an unordered component, with sorted roots and neighbours and cycles keyed on their node set so runs are deterministic and each cycle reports once. **PROVENANCE, stated rather than glossed: the dispatched agent was terminated mid-task by a session rate limit (HTTP 429) and never ran a single verification of its own** — its last emitted line was "Now the patch", yet its worktree already held a complete uncommitted patch. | T542 | Removed the docs/chore direct-commit exemption from `agents/orchestrator.md`, which instructed an action the remote refuses. **`ADR-007` branch 1 fires: an agent file contradicted `git-workflow.md`**, which is `maturity: stable` with `applyTo: "**"` and whose Rails block states it *"Does not grant any agent, including the orchestrator, authority to bypass branch protection for any reason, including docs-only or ledger-only changes."* **§5 does not obstruct**: removing a *permission to bypass* is the opposite of relaxing a check — the resulting contract is strictly harder to satisfy. **It was never a paper cut and had already caused the failure it describes:** `git-workflow.md`'s own Recovery Procedure records verbatim that the orchestrator committed ledger transitions directly onto `develop` **twice** and `git push origin develop` was rejected both times. **THE SUBSTANTIVE FINDING — the defect was three encodings in one section, not the two lines everyone located.** The brief, `plan-078` and `T535`'s report all placed it at lines 98–99. Verified pre-edit, the carve-out was granted three times: (1) the two routing bullets, the explicit grant; (2) **line 91's framing, "MANDATORY before any *code* commit"** — a docs commit is not a code commit, so the mandate **excluded docs by wording**; (3) **lines 102–103's "NEVER commit … *for feat/fix/refactor/test work*"** over an enumerated list **omitting docs/chore**, so by *expressio unius* a reader concludes they remain permitted. **A bare deletion of 98–99 would have left the exemption standing**, in a section titled "Branch Routing by Work Type" where silence on an enumerated type reads as "not covered" — which is the inference that produced the exemption in the first place. All three were fixed, and **that is why replace was chosen over delete**, on a better argument than the brief's: not "more useful to a reader" but *bare deletion would not have removed the defect.* **The restraint that keeps it a fix rather than a rival convention:** the new prose only restates, in the file's own routing vocabulary, what `git-workflow.md` already mandates, and **cites** it, so authority stays in one place — per branch 1's own reasoning that a command may specialise `AGENTS.md` but may not create a rival convention for the same thing. **It specifically refused to mint `docs/*` or `chore/*` branch prefixes**, verifying that `git-workflow.md` declares none, even though this repo's history uses `docs/…` branches extensively — **popularity is not authority**, and inventing the prefix from corpus practice would be exactly the rival convention branch 1 prevents; it says "use a descriptive branch name", matching the Recovery Procedure's own `<descriptive-branch-name>` placeholder. **Both mandated confirmations returned, including the negatives:** no third document grants the exemption — searched `exempt`, `docs-only`, `maintenance-only`, `commit directly`, `directly to develop` plus alternate phrasings; exactly one source-of-truth instance existed, every other hit being a generated projection or a document *reporting* this defect — and **two documents already pointed the correct way and needed no change** (`task-management/SKILL.md`: *"no exceptions for docs/ledger-only edits, including this one"*, and `release-manager.md`). **No propagation** to any other agent file, skill or command, so the copied-section hazard that hit `team-status.md` did not recur here. **A THIRD FINDING no gate covers, and it is systemic:** the **repo-root** installed projections were **not** regenerated by either generator — `sync.mjs` writes only under `implementation/` — and **`sync.mjs --check` reports "no drift across 577 files" regardless**, so `.claude/agents/orchestrator.md`, which is what the orchestrator actually running in this repo reads, **still contains the defect after this fix**. Independently corroborated: 5 of 19 root command projections already differed before `T536` touched anything. Scoped as `T543`. **Two brief corrections accepted:** `ADR-007`'s status is **`proposed`, not accepted** — which does not weaken the verdict, because the authority is `git-workflow.md` directly and `ADR-007` only supplies the tie-break procedure — and `ADR-007` is a *command*-contract ADR applied here to an *agent* file, an extension by analogy that branch 1's wording covers but which should be stated rather than glossed. Third, smaller: **`.cursor` projects agents as `.mdc`, not `.md`**, so any propagation sweep using `--include=*.md` misses Cursor. Gates: `tests/run.py` 824 OK / 23 skipped both sides; `check.py --registry` 166/0; `sync.mjs --check` no drift / 577 files; `check-maturity.py` 79/0 with `agent/experimental: 2` and `orchestrator`'s `maturity:` untouched; `scorecard.py --check` OK; `validate-tasks.py` PASS; `implementation/` verified fully purged of the exemption text | tech-lead, orchestrator | 2026-09-26 | `implementation/knowledge/agents/orchestrator.md` + 6 projections; `implementation/registry/index.json`; `docs/tasks/task-T542.md` |

| T578 | Adjudicated O1 (SECURITY:HIGH): the PoC security reviewer and poc-orchestrator sent critical findings, exposed secrets included, to debt handoff instead of blocking. Final ruling v3 has 12 edits. Blocking classes: any Immutable Constraint breach; any required control omitted, removed, disabled or weakened, at any severity; any CRITICAL or HIGH finding. These are never handed off as debt and are never waived. A severity floor keeps real credentials, injection paths and missing auth on non-public data at HIGH or above, and a presence check looks for required controls. MEDIUM findings need a remediation plan, due no later than handoff. The secrets check runs before every push carrying new commits and before every merge, at every step. Exposed-secret procedure: the user revokes the secret and reviews the issuer's audit log; the code fix is a normal commit; the user removes it from history, including the GitLab purge (Owner role); the reviewer re-checks with a redacted pattern scan. Open findings get a durable blocked task, so a stop or timeout cannot bury them, and the escalation literal is preserved. The v1 security review gave CONDITIONAL_PASS (7 MEDIUM). The v2 delta review gave CONDITIONAL_PASS (1 MEDIUM, 7 LOW), and v3 adopts both reviews verbatim. User decisions: the declined-rewrite path only under strict conditions; presence check accepted; QD strict. The orchestrator verified all 12 edits apply uniquely on c523415 with every phrase present. Order of application: A joint satisfiability (every pair, with a one-value check); B tier 1, meaning AGENTS.md and stable instructions within their declared scope, with lower documents amended toward them and an uncontested declared precedence holding inside tier 1, otherwise escalation; C a command's declared output contract governs (skills and agents may add, never override, and a command is never amended on their account); D peer self-placement, where only a document's own text placing the subject with the other decides; E escalate.
